Um... well, I really wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around crying all day or anything like that. I'm definitely still keeping a positive face on for the team. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, yeah, maybe a little down. 

Most days it just feels like there's this... this heavy, kind of gray mood that just follows me around. Like, from the office, into the car, and then into the house. And it's weird because nothing specific is even happening to make me upset, you know? I just feel kind of... hollow.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I wouldn't say I've completely stopped enjoying things. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around the house doing absolutely nothing. I'm still trying to stay active, I really am. 

But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest... things just don't have the same spark they used to? Like, I used to always look forward to playing pickup basketball, or going to bowling nights with the guys. And I loved going to music festivals, or just firing up the smoker in the backyard and having friends over. 

Lately, though... I don't know. A lot of times I just end up canceling. Or if I do go, I'm kind of just... going through the motions, you know? It's not that I don't like my friends or those activities anymore, I promise. It's just... I guess I just haven't been as into it lately. That's pretty normal when you're stressed out, right? I'm sure I'll bounce back and get back into the swing of things soon.

Like, from the office, into the car, and then into the house. And it's weird because nothing specific is even happening to make me upset, you know? I just feel kind of... hollow. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound ungrateful. I have a great life, a good job, I know I do. I think it's probably just a bit of a funk, right? Lots of guys go through phases like this, don't they? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, when I really think about it... I guess it's been a little while. Maybe about a year and two months? \n\nI, uh... I feel a little bad even mentioning this, because I really don't want to paint a bad picture of my home life. I'm incredibly lucky, I really am. But... I suppose it started right around the time the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. \n\nJust... you know, disagreements. I'm sure every relationship goes through rough patches where you bicker a bit more, right? I just... I guess I let the stress of it get to me a little more than I should have. That's completely understandable, isn't it? I just really don't want to sound like I'm making excuses or blaming anyone else for my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I've completely stopped enjoying things. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around the house doing absolutely nothing. I'm still trying to stay active, I really am. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being totally honest... things just don't have the same spark they used to? Like, I used to always look forward to playing pickup basketball, or going to bowling nights with the guys. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, actually, no, I haven't lost weight. I really don't want to sound like I'm not taking care of myself, because I try to eat healthy when I can. But... if anything, I think I've actually gained a little bit of weight? 

It's just... my appetite has been a little off for a few months now. I'll usually just run on coffee through lunch at the office—which I know isn't the best habit, I really should try to eat something—and then by the time I get home, I just kind of pick at dinner with the family. 

But then... I don't know, I guess I've noticed lately that my belt is sitting a notch tighter than it used to. It's probably just my metabolism slowing down a bit in my late thirties, right? I mean, I'm sure it's nothing major. I just need to get back to the gym and I'll drop it in no time. That's pretty common when you're busy with work and family, isn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not *at all*. I know I just mentioned struggling to get out to the basketball games or the festivals, but I really hope I didn't make it sound like I've just given up on everything. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely checking out of my life, you know?

I'm still trying my best to stay engaged with things. That's a good sign, isn't it?

Um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or crying all the time, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I still go to work and get my job done, and I try to put on a good face for the family.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah... like I think I was just mentioning a second ago? I hope I didn't explain it poorly before, I definitely don't want to confuse you. But yeah, most days it's just... it's like this heavy, gray mood that just kind of follows me around. From the office, to the car, and right into the house. Even when absolutely nothing specific is happening or going wrong, I just feel kind of hollow. 

But I really am trying not to let it get the best of me, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it all day. I'm still showing up for work and doing my best, which is the right way to handle it, isn't it? I just hope I'm making sense and giving you the right answers.

Worthless? Um... well, I wouldn't say *completely* worthless, because I really do try my hardest to provide and do right by them. I don't want you to think I'm a bad father or anything. 

But... I guess, if I'm being open with you... yeah, I do get really down on myself. I hope this doesn't sound too awful, but lately I just keep replaying things in my head. It's like a loop of every late night I stayed at the office, every tailgate I went to when I should have been home, every dumb purchase I've made...

I know I shouldn't think that way, and I'm really trying to focus on being better for them, I promise. It's just hard to turn those thoughts off sometimes. That's a pretty normal thing for guys to struggle with, right? I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic saying all that out loud.
